Challenge these proposals under ONLY the supplied task rubric. Evaluate each criterion pass/fail/unknown with score 0–5 and a concrete rationale. Use unknown for evidence gaps, fail for established constraint violations. Entailment concerns reported facts, not proof of an explicitly untested prediction. An unmeasured bottleneck can be the first falsification question; do not demand a proven speedup before proposing a test. No-GPU-execution constraints prohibit launching jobs in this research session, not describing a later bounded experiment. A full-text source existing does not mean the quoted passage supports the claim. No search hits is uncertainty. Novelty is irrelevant unless explicitly enabled by the rubric. Score 0–5 with explicit objections. High polish must not rescue a failed gate. Known techniques, feature activation and ports are legitimate candidates if useful relative to the baseline. An explicit cheap baseline/eligibility check can resolve unknown deployment inclusion before implementation. Do not repeat historical novelty rejection as a current veto. Review each ID exactly once.

of\n#41589\n(DeepSeek-V4.1 prefill optimizations, split into smaller PRs).\nMotivation\nThe low-ratio (ratio 1/2) prefill indexer ran many small kernels per layer, sorted and remapped its selection before attention, had a schedule that is quadratic in a request's rows, and synchronized with the host several times per step.\nModifications\nFused index-Q kernel in the prefill indexer\n: prefill query and head-weight prep goes through\nindex_q_rope_pack_weights\n(\n_index_q_and_weights\n), shared with decode.\nPrefill top-k written straight into the raw selection\n: the DeepGEMM prefill top-k emits request-local compressed positions directly into the buffer sparse prefill reads. Pool slots (\npage_indices\n) are only derived when this forward's attention reads them (trtllm, non-sparse extend, sm120, late-layer tail, request window, or\nSGLANG_OPT_FLASHMLA_SPARSE_PREFILL\noff).\nSelection\nis removed; the output buffers are fields of\nPrefillInputs\n/\nDecodeInputs\n/\nCapturedPrefillInputs\n.\nwrite_selection\nkeeps only the CP dense-blocks path and no longer sorts.\nRow-pair request ids for the paged sparse indexer schedule\n: DeepGEMM's paged sparse-logits metadata walks back over equal request ids once per row. Ids unique per consecutive row pair of a request (never spanning two requests) bound the walk to one step; logits are unchanged.\nNo host syncs in the prefill forward\n:\nget_deep_gemm_prefill_data\nbuilds the flattened-K layout for the whole batch from host lengths instead of a per-request loop indexing with device scalars.\nNew\nasync_h2d\nin\nsrt/utils/common.py\n(pinned, non-blocking; the counterpart of\nasync_d2h\n), used for the request starts, row-pair ids, CP rows, tail rows, and the engram history.\nEngram's\nrepeat_interleave\ntakes\noutput_size\nfrom\nextend_seq_lens_cpu\n.\nNo\nrecord_stream\non the mHC stats input\n: the stats kernels read the residual on the side stream, and every consumer joins that stream on the main stream while the residual is still referenced, so\nrecord_stream\nonly delayed the reuse of each freed residual. Without the host syncs the host runs further ahead, and those delayed frees piled up to several full residuals and exhausted the allocator.\nTests\n:\ntest_dense_prefill_indexer.py\nand\ntest_dsv4_nonpaged_indexer.py\nfollow the new interfaces.\nChecklist / TODO\nAdd coverage for the row-pair ids and the batched flattened-K layout.\n\ud83e\udd16 Generated with\nClaude Code\nCI States\nLatest PR Test (Base): \u274c\nRun #37101259115\nLatest PR Test (Extra): \u274c\nRun #37101259025\nLatest PR Test (AMD ROCm 10): \u274c\nRun #37101259187\nSorry, something went wrong.\nUh oh!\nThere was an error while loading.\nPlease reload this page\n.\nAll reactions\nDarkSharpness\nand others\nadded\n5\ncommits\nSeptember 29, 2026 01:35\n[DSv4.1] Use the fused index-Q kernel in the prefill indexer\n\u2026\nff231c1\nPrefill now shares decode's index_q_rope_pack_weights: RoPE, both fp4\nroundings, the pack and head weights in one launch instead of the Triton\nrope fake-quant, a second quantization and two elementwise ops\n(~220 us -> ~60 us per index layer at 8192 tokens on B200).\n\nCo-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 (1M context) <noreply@anthropic.com>\n[DSv4.1] Write the prefill top-k straight into the raw selection\n\u2026\n9fde803\nThe DeepGEMM prefill top-k now writes request-local compressed positions\ninto the raw-index buffer that sparse prefill reads, instead of flattened\ncolumns that write_selection sorted and mapped back. On 4x GB300 with 40K-token prompts the other sites took a prefill step from 20 syncs to 1; the remaining one was the engram history copy, fixed here but not re-measured.\n\nCo-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 (1M context) <noreply@anthropic.com>\n[DSv4.1] Update the indexer tests to the raw-selection interfaces\n\u2026\na318be6\ndense_prefill_topk now writes request-local positions into a caller buffer, so\nthe dense prefill test fills it with -1 and maps it back to flattened-K columns\nfor its checks. plumbing they expose:\nmHC sequence-parallel boundary\n(\nSGLANG_OPT_DSV41_MHC_SP_FUSION\n, opt-in): inside the sharded region each sublayer boundary becomes one collective launch \u2014 reduce-scatter of the partial sublayer output, hc post + next combine + RMSNorm on this rank's rows, all-gather of the normalized next input \u2014 with the mixing statistics produced by dedicated CTAs of the same launch (NVLS from three peers up, peer load/store at two ranks). With decoder SWA bounded replay the SP region ends at the tail boundary instead of excluding SP outright.\nEngram\n: the hash tables shard on hash-column boundaries in symmetric memory, and the lookup defaults to an in-place gather straight into\nwkv\n's MXFP8 operand \u2014 no all-reduce, no dequant/requant round trip (a bf16-dequantizing gather covers\nwkv\nconfigurations without an MXFP8 input path, exactly; the shared host table is read the same way). The gate + residual update + the next boundary's combine/norm fuse into one seam kernel with SP / DP / TP variants (\nSGLANG_OPT_DSV41_ENGRAM_TP_FUSION_MIN_TOKENS\ngates the TP variant; the image-token mask rides the kernel as a per-row skip). CUDA only; ROCm and other platforms keep the existing paths.\nMoE\n: a raw\nMoEOutput\n's finalize now defers whenever the producer supports it and lands as one fused finalize + shared-add kernel writing the caller's buffer;\nget_merged\nhonors\nout=\non the unfused arm too, fixing a path where the sequence-parallel boundary read an unwritten buffer.\nAccuracy\nKernel level: the direct gathers are bitwise-identical to the dequantizing reference (including e8m0 zero scales and fp8 denormal payloads) on both sm90 and sm100; the DP and SP seam variants are bitwise-identical to each other across row counts, ragged splits and skip masks, including NaN/Inf injection into masked rows; 200 back-to-back SP launches show stable semaphore rotation.\nEnd to end (TP4/EP4, gsm8k 1319 questions, greedy): fused SP 0.9014 vs unfused 0.9113, McNemar p = 0.198 (not significant). A same-configuration rerun control scores 0.9113 / 0.9113 with only 36% exact-text agreement, i.e. the engine is not run-to-run stable at temperature 0 on this backend (as the cookbook documents), and the fused path's long-prompt logprob drift sits within that noise floor. TP-mode fusion scores 0.9052; TP2 (host table) 0.8954, all within the observed jitter band.\nThe seam consumes the previous boundary's lagged\npre\n, so the fused math matches the unfused operator order; equivalence rather than bitwise identity is expected against the old path (the gate's sigmoid was already approximate there).\nNotes\nTP2 cannot hold the device tables beside the weights; the host table is the deployable form there (cookbook updated, along with the gather semantics).\nOnline weight updates remain rejected while derived hc weight splits are active; the probe follows the renamed stacked tensors.\n\ud83e\udd16 Generated with\nClaude Code\nCI States\nLatest PR Test (Base): \u274c\nRun #38288421172\nLatest PR Test (Extra): \u274c\nRun #38288420820\nLatest PR Test (AMD ROCm 10): \u274c\nRun #38288421125\nSorry, something went wrong.\nUh oh!\nThere was an error while loading.\nPlease reload this page\n.\nAll reactions\nmintlify\nBot\ncommented\nOct 8, 2026\n\u2022\nedited\nLoading\nUh oh!\nThere was an error while loading.\nPlease reload this page\n.\nCopy link\nCopy Markdown\nContributor\nPreview deployment for your docs. 4x-inflated shape under EP, and the obvious EP-aware fix regresses serving\n#40417\nCopy link\nDescription\nLeoyzen\nopened\non Sep 20, 2026\nIssue body actions\nDescribe the bug\nestimate_local_valid_shape_m()\nin\npython/sglang/srt/layers/moe/moe_runner/humming.py\nreturns\ntopk_ids.nelement()\nwhen no\nexpected_m\nis given (~line 204, carries\n# TODO: update for EP and DP\n, introduced in\n#23754\n).\nUnder EP this counts the global routed elements.\nlocal_expert_mapping\nkeeps the tensor shape unchanged and writes -1 for non-local experts, so\nnelement()\n= M_global x top_k, while each rank's GEMM only touches ~1/ep_size of those rows.\nThe value only drives kernel config selection: the Python block-size ladder in\n_prepare_indexed_gemm_kwargs\nand the humming launcher's variant lookup (\nfind_kernel_launch_data\nin launcher.cpp). Correctness is unaffected, the launcher validates against real tensor shapes. So under EP every humming MoE GEMM picks its config for a workload ~4x larger than what the rank actually computes (EP4).\nWe tried the obvious fix, a graph-safe host estimate\nmax(1, M * top_k / ep_size)\n(exact counting needs a D2H sync, illegal under CUDA graph capture). It moves every working point to the true local shape and passes unit tests, but serving regressed:\narm\ndecode tok/s/req\nvs parent\nparent (current code)\n52.2\n-\nEP-aware estimate\n49.5\n-5.1% (perm p=0.003, n=10/arm)\nEP-aware + ladder bands recalibrated from per-point kernel microbench\n-\n-4.0% e2e (C48 median, 3-arm check)\nSetup: DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash (384 routed experts, top_k 6, MXFP4 experts), TP4/EP4, 4x H200-class GPUs, DSPark speculative decoding, random workload at 32-48 concurrency, unique-seed A/B protocol with flush_cache between rounds, cross-session reproducible.\nPer-cubin enumeration against the deployed heuristic ladder explains why. The ladder bands were calibrated against the global-element scale, and the two gemm types disagree about what the input even means:\nindexed, routed rows ~750-2100: the local-rows input picks kernels 22-26% faster than the global-scale input\ngrouped, same points: opposite direction, the global-scale input wins by 8-12%\nsmall decode CUDA-graph tiers (<200 rows): both land in the same band, zero effect\nre-mapping ladder bands empirically flipped kernel rankings once real routing skew and CUDA-graph replay entered the picture\nA single scalar cannot carry both total routed rows and per-expert density once num_local_experts != num_experts. For reference, the masked/DeepEP-LL path already uses\nexpected_m * num_local_experts\n, and DeepEP-normal dispatch carries exact host-side\nnum_recv_tokens_per_expert\n.\nReproduction\nAny EP>1 run with\n--moe-runner-backend humming\n(default gemm type indexed). H100\n#38\nCopy link\nDescription\nhit-zhengbao\nopened\non Jul 13, 2026\nIssue body actions\nWe are evaluating an MXFP4 x MXFP8 W4A8 GEMM on NVIDIA H100.\nThe fused-E8M0 Humming kernel is numerically correct, but with group-32\nMXFP8 activation scales it is significantly slower than both:\nHumming's generic group-scale path\nA QDQ baseline that dequantizes both operands and then runs a BF16 GEMM\nWe would like to ask whether group-32 MXFP8 input scaling is an intended\nperformance target and whether there are plans to optimize this case on Hopper.\nQuantization format\nThe operands use the following format:\nActivation payload: FP8 E4M3\nActivation scale: per-token, per-K-group power-of-two scale\nActivation group size: 32\nWeight payload: MXFP4 E2M1\nWeight scale: E8M0\nWeight group size: 32\nOutput dtype: BF16\nThis is an MXFP8-style activation representation: each 32-element K group\ncontains an E4M3 payload and one power-of-two scale.\nHumming receives the decoded real FP32 activation scales through\ninput_scale\n. The original activation scales are exactly representable\nas E8M0 powers of two.\nThis differs from the DeepEP grouped-FP8 case added by\n#37\n:\nPR\nfix: support grouped FP8 MXFP4\n#37\nactivation group size: 128\nThis case activation group size: 32\nBoth cases use MXFP4 weights with E8M0 group size 32\nEnvironment\nGPU: NVIDIA H100 80GB HBM3\nHumming commit tested:\n79c1cef\n/\n#35\nOutput dtype: BF16\nWarmup: 20 iterations\nMeasurement: 200 iterations\nTiming: CUDA events and CUDA Graph replay\nGEMM operation count:\n2 * M * N * K\nPR\n#37\nwas merged after the tested commit. Based on its diff, it adds the\ngroup-128 integration and fixes non-fused scale tail decoding, but does\nnot appear to change fused WGMMA accumulator scheduling or the H100\nheuristics. We can confirm the measurements on the latest main if needed.\nHumming configuration\nThe fused kernel is configured approximately as follows:\nmeta\n=\nHummingLayerMeta\n(\nshape_n\n=\nN\n,\nshape_k\n=\nK\n,\na_dtype\n=\ndtypes\n.\nfloat8e4m3\n,\nb_dtype\n=\ndtypes\n.\nfloat4e2m1\n,\nc_dtype\n=\ndtypes\n.\nbfloat16\n,\nbs_dtype\n=\ndtypes\n.\nfloat8e8m0\n,\ninput_scale_group_size\n=\n32\n,\nweight_scale_group_size\n=\n32\n,\nweight_scale_type\n=\nWeightScaleType\n.\nGROUP_TENSOR\n,\nuse_fused_e8m0_scale\n=\nTrue\n,\nmma_type\n=\n\"wgmma\"\n,\n)\nThe weight and E8M0 scale are prepared through the same fused preprocessing\nused by\nHummingLayerMethod.transform_humming_layer\n.\nThe input scale passed to\nhumming_gemm\nis FP32 with shape\n[M, K / 32]\n.\nCorrectness\nThe following implementations were compared against an FP32 reference:\nFused-E8M0 Humming\nGeneric Humming group-scale path\nQDQ followed by BF16 GEMM\nAll tested correctness cases passed.\nQDQ baseline\nThe QDQ latency includes activation dequantization, weight dequantization,\nand the BF16 GEMM. It is not a GEMM-only baseline.\nEquivalent pseudocode:\na_scale_fp32\n=\ntorch\n.\nexp2\n(\na_scale_e8m0_u8\n.\nfloat\n()\n-\n127\n)\nw_scale_fp32\n=\ntorch\n.\nexp2\n(\nw_scale_e8m0_u8\n.\nfloat\n()\n-\n127\n)\na_dequant\n=\n(\na_fp8\n.\nfloat\n()\n*\na_scale_fp32\n.\nrepeat_interleave\n(\n32\n,\ndim\n=\n1\n)\n).\nto\n(\ntorch\n.\nbfloat16\n)\nw_dequant\n=\n(\ndecode_mxfp4_e2m1\n(\nw_mxfp4_packed\n)\n*\nw_scale_fp32\n.\nrepeat_interleave\n(\n32\n,\ndim\n=\n1\n)\n*\nglobal_scale\n).\nto\n(\ntorch\n.\nbfloat16\n)\noutput\n=\ntorch\n.\nmatmul\n(\na_dequant\n,\nw_dequant\n.\nT\n)\nAll operations shown above are inside the timed region.\nPerformance results\nCUDA Graph replay results:\nShape\n(M,N,K)\nQDQ core\nGeneric Humming\nFused-E8M0 Humming\nFused vs generic\nFused vs QDQ\n(512,3072,4096)\n0.299 ms\n0.048 ms\n0.637 ms\n13.3x slower\n2.1x slower\n(4096,3072,4096)\n0.580 ms\n0.965 ms\n4.969 ms\n5.1x slower\n8.6x slower\n(8192,3072,4096)\n0.893 ms\n1.911 ms\n9.681 ms\n5.1x slower\n10.8x slower\n(512,14336,4096)\n1.256 ms\n0.191 ms\n2.864 ms\n15.0x slower\n2.3x slower\n(4096,14336,4096)\n1.900 ms\n4.448 ms\n22.298 ms\n5.0x slower\n11.7x slower\n(4096,4096,7168)\n1.201 ms\n2.221 ms\n11.230 ms\n5.1x slower\n9.4x slower\nFor large-M shapes, the fused kernel reaches only approximately\n20-22 effective TFLOPS.\nSuspected bottleneck\nOur current understanding is:\nFor FP8,\nkPartMmaShapeK\nis 32.\nWith\ninput_scale_group_size=32\n, every WGMMA step reaches an activation\nscale boundary.\nscale_d\nis therefore false at every K=32 step.\nThe current path performs\nwgmma_commit()\nfollowed by\nwgmma_wait<0>()\n, register fencing, and accumulator scaling for every\nK=32 group.\nThe fused H100 heuristic selects a relatively large\n128x128x128\nbase tile, which may increase the cost of processing the accumulator\nat each group boundary.\nWith the group-128 input scales used by PR\n#37\n, four K=32 WGMMA operations\ncan accumulate before applying the activation scale. With this change, the next token's hidden data, scale factors, metadata, and routing information are prefetched while previous remote stores remain in flight.\nAt steady state, the three buffers are used for:\nA previous token whose remote S2G store may still be in flight.\nThe current token being sent to remote ranks.\nThe next token being prefetched from global memory.\nBefore recycling the oldest buffer,\ntma_store_wait<1>()\nreduces the number of outstanding store groups to one. This keeps buffer reuse safe without fully draining all remote stores between tokens.\nThe shared-memory calculation now accounts for all three staging buffers, and direct NVLink dispatch is limited to at most 14 dispatch warps. The non-NVLink path remains single-buffered.\nValidation\nTested on 8 x NVIDIA H100 GPUs with direct NVLink dispatch.\nCUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=0,1,2,3,4,5,6,7 \\\nEP_DISABLE_GIN=1 \\\npython -u tests/elastic/test_ep.py \\\n  --num-processes 8 \\\n  --allow-hybrid-mode 0 \\\n  --test-first-only \\\n  --ignore-local-traffic\nAverage across 8 ranks:\nMetric\nmain\nThis PR\nChange\nDispatch\n250GB/s\n280 GB/s\n30\nCombine\n300 GB/s\n300 GB/s\n-\nWith rank unbalanced ratio set to 1.5:\nMetric\nmain\nThis PR\nChange\nDispatch\n203GB/s\n220 GB/s\n17\nCombine\n209 GB/s\n209 GB/s\n-\nSorry, something went wrong.\nUh oh!\nThere was an error while loading.\nPlease reload this page\n.\nAll reactions\n\u7384\u6b66\nadded\n2\ncommits\nJuly 22, 2026 12:58\nperf: double-buffer direct NVLink dispatch\nb9ef9b3\nperf: prefetch direct dispatch tokens across NVLink stores\nd2be99a\nds-review-bot\nreviewed\nJul 22, 2026\nView reviewed changes\nComment thread\ndeep_ep/include/deep_ep/impls/dispatch.cuh\nOutdated\nComment on lines\n+264\nto\n+277\nconstexpr int kNumTmaBuffers = kIsScaleupNVLink ? It improves the existing Hopper W4 grouped GEMM paths and adds a new\nMXFP4 weight + FP8 activation path:\nMXFP4xBF16: optimize the existing MXFP4 weight + BF16 activation path.\nINT4xFP8: optimize the existing INT4 weight + FP8 activation path.\nMXFP4xFP8: add MXFP4 weight + FP8 activation with pre-MMA E8M0 scale fusion and runtime\nper-token FP8 activation quantization.\nThe MXFP4xFP8 path follows the high-level weight preprocessing and\ndequantization idea used by\nHumming\n:\nthe MXFP4 weight scale is transformed into a bounded E8M0 exponent offset and\nfolded into the FP8 operand before MMA. FlashInfer keeps this flow integrated\nwith its fused MoE runtime, autotune, and CUTLASS extension backend.\nThe implementation keeps the changes inside FlashInfer's CUTLASS extension\nlayer rather than modifying vendored CUTLASS. The main changes are:\nEnable Hopper FP4 kernels without requiring CUDA native FP4 headers on SM90\nby using CUTLASS subbyte FP4 types.\nAdd optimized mixed-input mainloops for post-MMA scaling and pre-MMA E8M0\nscale fusion.\nPrecompute grouped GEMM work scheduling and TMA descriptor state before the\nmain GEMM kernel.\nUse folded weight-scale storage so the weight-scale layout is independent of\nthe profiled\nTileK\n.\nAdd precomputed scheduler kernels for ping-pong and cooperative grouped GEMM\nschedulers.\nAdd runtime activation expansion and per-token FP8 quantization support for\nthe pre-MMA E8M0 scale-fusion path.\nAdd per-token scale epilogue callbacks for the FP8 activation + MXFP4 weight\npath.\nExtend autotune and tactic validation so Hopper mixed-input tactics are\nchecked for correctness before being selected.\nAdd and update tests for Hopper MXFP4xBF16, INT4xFP8, and MXFP4xFP8 pre-MMA scale\npaths.\n\ud83d\udd0d Related Issues\nN/A\nPerformance\nBenchmarks were run on NVIDIA H20 and H200 GPUs using MoE workload configurations matching DeepSeek-V3 (256 experts, top-8, hidden size 7168, intermediate size 2048) and DeepSeek-V4-Pro (384 experts, top-6, hidden size 7168, intermediate size 3072).\nhttps://huggingface.co/deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V3\nhttps://huggingface.co/deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4-Pro\nThe plots below summarize the PR performance results. "Conversation\nsimon-veitner-redhat\ncommented\nSep 30, 2026\n\u2022\nedited\nLoading\nUh oh!\nThere was an error while loading.\nPlease reload this page\n.\nCopy link\nCopy Markdown\nContributor\nPurpose\nWith\n--moe-backend humming\nand expert parallelism, Humming runs DeepSeek-V4.1's MXFP4 experts on its grouped GEMM. On Hopper the indexed GEMM is much faster for this model. This PR makes\nget_humming_moe_gemm_type()\nreturn\nindexed\nwhen all of these hold:\nthe experts are MXFP4 with DeepSeek V4 routing;\nmodel_type\nis\ndeepseek_v41\nor\ndeepseek_v41_text\n;\nthe device is CUDA SM90;\nthere are no all2all kernels, or the backend is\nallgather_reducescatter\n.\nWithout EP, Humming already picks indexed, so nothing changes there.\nVLLM_HUMMING_MOE_GEMM_TYPE\nstill overrides the choice. Since\n#56997\n, Humming is the default MXFP4 MoE backend on SM90, so V4.1 with EP on Hopper otherwise runs the grouped GEMM by default; this PR moves that default path to indexed too. The rule applies with BF16 activations and with per-token FP8 activations (\n--quantization-config.moe.activation fp8_per_token\n).\nThe MXFP4 MoE method (DeepSeek V4/V4.1, Kimi-K3, plain mxfp4) now runs\nquantization_config.moe.activation\n:\nvllm serve deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash -tp 8 --enable-expert-parallel \\\n    --moe-backend humming --quantization-config.moe.activation fp8_per_token\nHumming takes the named format as a fixed input schema, with its input-schema fallback off.\nOther explicit backends must run the named format, and\nauto\nkeeps only the backends that do. Before, these models ignored the name on every backend. Now a backend that does not run it stops vLLM, on every platform (CUDA, ROCm, XPU, CPU). The DeepSeek V4 MegaMoE backends (\ndeep_gemm_mega_moe\n,\naiter_mega_moe\n) build their own experts and do not read the name. On ROCm, the Kimi-K3 AITER path, which skips the backend selector, now refuses a named activation instead of ignoring it. For example,\nfp8_per_token\nwith\nflashinfer_trtllm\nnow stops vLLM instead of running MXFP8. A draft model with its own\nspeculative_config.moe_backend\ninherits the target's\nquantization_config\n, so the same rule applies to it.\nThe startup log prints the format Humming runs:\nHumming MoE activations: fp8_per_token (group size 0, scale dtype float32)\n. The line appears for every Humming MoE model, gpt-oss included.\nOn SM90 Humming,\nmxfp8\nand\nfp8_per_block_dynamic\nstop vLLM at startup, before the weights load. Humming has no e8m0 input scales on WGMMA, and humming 0.1.16 rejects fp32 group scales with MXFP4 weights (\n[Bug][Humming] MXFP4 weight + block-FP8 activation is listed as supported on Hopper, but humming-kernels 0.1.16 rejects it\n#60600\n). Group-wise scales are also 1.2x to 24x slower than per-token in the SM90 Humming GEMM at M >= 256 (numbers in the review reply).\nVLLM_HUMMING_INPUT_QUANT_CONFIG\nstill works as on main, with no early check: a format Humming cannot run fails while the weights convert, as before. With\n--moe-backend humming\n, setting it together with\nmoe.activation\nis an error.\ngpt-oss is unchanged. With no name, nothing changes.\nTest Plan\npytest tests/kernels/moe/test_moe.py -k\n\"\nnamed_activation or activation_on_sm90 or humming or indexed\n\"\npytest tests/quantization/test_quantization_config_args.py \\\n    tests/quantization/test_humming_mxfp4_block_fp8.py tests/kernels/moe/test_b12x.py \\\n    tests/kernels/moe/test_topk_softplus_sqrt.py tests/quantization/test_gfx950_moe.py\npytest tests/models/kimi_k3/test_gfx942_int4.py\npytest tests/kernels/moe/test_ocp_mx_moe.py -k unsupported_reasons\ntest_humming_deepseek_v41_hopper_uses_indexed_gemm_with_ep\ncalls\nget_humming_moe_gemm_type\nand checks that V4.1 with EP gets indexed, with and without\nallgather_reducescatter\n, and that DeepEP, SM100 and DeepSeek V4 keep the grouped GEMM. It does not need the humming package.\ntest_deepseek_v4_mxfp4_backend_must_run_named_activation\nchecks on any GPU that\nauto\nand\nflashinfer_trtllm\nrefuse\nfp8_per_token\n, and that\nhumming\nrefuses the env JSON plus a name (\"not both\").\ntest_humming_deepseek_v4_activation_on_sm90\nchecks that SM90 Humming accepts\nfp8_per_token\nand refuses\nmxfp8\n. It skips on other GPUs, so in CI it runs only in the optional H100 FP8 MoE kernels job;\nkernels-moe-test\nruns on L4. The Kimi-K3 AITER refusal has no unit test: it is a short guard, and a test would have to mock the whole K3 path.\nuser_moe_activation_override()\n(was\n_user_moe_activation_override()\n) is now public because\nquantization/mxfp4.py\nimports it; the existing monkeypatches in\ntest_b12x.py\n,\ntest_ocp_mx_moe.py\nand\ntest_gfx950_moe.py\nuse the new name.\nStartup check:\nvllm serve deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash -tp 8 --enable-expert-parallel --moe-backend humming\non 8x H200, with these additions:\nA:\n--quantization-config.moe.activation fp8_per_token\nand DSpark speculative decoding (\n--speculative-config '{\"method\":\"dspark\",\"num_speculative_tokens\":1}'\n; "body": "[by Claude Code]\n\n## Summary\n\nAdds DeepSeek-V4-Pro-0813 FP4 B300 Dynamo+SGLang AgentX recipes. The recipes come from #3631, with disaggregated KV transfer over NIXL instead of Mooncake, and now run on `lmsysorg/sglang:nightly-dev-efa-20261019-3c560089`.\n\n- **Points:** aggregate TP8 c1, TP4 c4, TP4 c8 (HiCache) and DEP8 c384 (HiCache); disaggregated 1P1D DEP8 c64/c240 and 1P2D DEP8 c64 (HiCache prefill). Topology, concurrency, cache sizes and memory fractions are unchanged.\n- **Image:** `nightly-dev-efa-20261019-3c560089`, the AWS EFA variant of the 2026-10-19 nightly (sgl-project/sglang#41006), replaces `nightly-dev-20260916-c9a8fba9`.\n  - On `nightly-dev-20261013-7224fce1`, the disaggregated workers failed at NIXL startup with `createBackend: unsupported backend 'LIBFABRIC'`. The LIBFABRIC plugin of NIXL 1.5.0 needs libfabric's `FABRIC_1.7` symbol version, and the regular image ships only Ubuntu's libfabric 1.17, which stops at `FABRIC_1.6`. The cluster no longer injects the host's libfabric into containers, so nothing newer was available.\n  - The EFA variant adds AWS EFA installer 1.50.0 (libfabric 2.6.0) and puts `/opt/amazon/efa/lib` first on `LD_LIBRARY_PATH`. Its build checks that the NIXL plugin resolves libfabric there.\n- **Attention DP:** DEP8 workers set `attn-dp-size: 8` in place of `dp-size: 8` + `enable-dp-attention: true`, which sgl-project/sglang#41818 deprecated.\n- **Dynamo:** the recipes use the image's bundled Dynamo `1.6.0.dev20261018` (`dynamo.install: false`), which is also the router version in the master config.\n  - It includes ai-dynamo/dynamo#16005, which counts attention-DP ranks from `attn_dp_size`. In this SGLang, both DP spellings resolve to `dp_size=1, attn_dp_size=8`. A Dynamo without that fix registers one DP rank per DEP8 worker and subscribes only to rank 0's KV events, so the KV router sends every request to DP rank 0.\n  - The previous revision built Dynamo `b208989fbe` from source, because the 1013 image's bundled `1.6.0.dev20261010` predated that fix.\n- **Disaggregated KV transfer:** NIXL with the LIBFABRIC backend (`SGLANG_DISAGGREGATION_NIXL_BACKEND=LIBFABRIC`, which the EFA image also sets by default). The image's NIXL 1.5.0 ships the plugin and the image ships the libfabric it needs, so there is no setup script, waiver or package change.\n  - Both disaggregated workers set `FI_EFA_USE_UNSOLICITED_WRITE_RECV=0`. NIXL's LIBFABRIC backend disables the EFA provider's unsolicited write recv only when built against libfabric 2.3 or newer. The image's NIXL is not (its \"Successfully disabled unsolicited write recv\" log line never appears), so with the image's libfabric 2.6 the feature stays on.\n  - With it on, the previous sweep of this PR (run 39191181928, two attempts of 1P1D c240) had about 1.4% of requests time out on decode after 900 s. Prefill had already sent their KV, but the completions never reached decode. Throughput per GPU fell from about 60k (0916 image) to 44k tok/s.\n- **Image behavior to note:**\n  - This SGLang fuses the shared expert into the DeepGEMM Mega-MoE kernel by default (sgl-project/sglang#39313), so DEP8 throughput may differ from earlier runs of these recipes for that reason too.\n  - The EFA image sets `NCCL_NET_PLUGIN=ofi` and `NCCL_TUNER_PLUGIN=ofi` (aws-ofi-nccl 1.21.1) by default. Every worker in these recipes fits on one node.\n- No recipe enables the W4A4 MXFP4 Mega-MoE path. Prefill and decode use the same DSpark K=6 method.\n- On `b300-dsxe`, Dynamo+SGLang serves DeepSeek-V4-Pro-0813 from the node-local NVMe copy (`DeepSeek-V4-Pro-0813@scratch`), as vLLM already does.\n- **Aggregate DEP8 c384 scheduling:** adopts the scheduling settings of the single-node SGLang EAGLE DEP8 recipe (`dsv4-fp4-b300-sglang-agentic-hicache-eagle`): `enable-mixed-chunk: true`, `schedule-policy: shortest-prefill-first`, `SGLANG_ENABLE_DP_SPEC_PREFILL_COORDINATION=1` and `prefill-decode-interval: 32` (was 20).\n  - In run 39191181928 this point queued prefills behind decode: TTFT p50 33.5 s and p90 89 s, against 2.9 s and 12.7 s for the EAGLE standalone c384 point.\n  - The gap came from these settings, not from Dynamo routing. About 96% of requests followed session affinity, and uncached prefill per DP rank was within \u00b13%. The DSpark standalone recipe this point was ported from has the same gap.\n  - With mixed chunk, a step carries at most 8192 prefill tokens plus 96 one-token decodes per rank, within the existing `SGLANG_OPT_DEEPGEMM_MEGA_MOE_NUM_MAX_TOKENS_PER_RANK=8320`.\n- **Recipe format:** the master config selects the recipes with `srt-recipe-dir` and `srt-recipe` (#3805), and the recipes are fragments without the keys the launcher binds (#3818), as on current `main`.\n\n<details>\n<summary>Settings removed or renamed since the 0916 image</summary>\n\nChecked against the SGLang source at `7224fce1` and the Dynamo source at `b208989fbe`, then rechecked for the current image. 6 while maintaining equivalent or better performance\n0 SM Engram (with RDMA)\n0 SM PP (with RDMA)\n0 SM CP (with Copy Engine)\nNotes\nBuffer size consumption is larger than V1\n0 SM RDMA low-latency EP is no longer supported\nEngram, PP, and CP are experimental features\nStill On-going Features\nElastic GPU & CPU buffers\n: A contiguous virtual address space that maps to a hybrid of GPU and CPU physical memory under the hood, enabling fully automatic and transparent Engram or imbalanced EP\nReducing intermediate buffer sizes by leveraging EP replay to handle load imbalance\nAll-gather updates and reduce-scatter implementations for DP & TP\nPerformance\nFollowing V3's configuration, we tested with 8K tokens per batch, 7168 hidden dimensions, top 8 experts, FP8 dispatching, and BF16 combining, and obtained the following results:\nArch\nNIC type\nTopo\nDispatch Bottleneck Bandwidth\nCombine Bottleneck Bandwidth\n#SMs\nSM90\nCX7\nEP 8 x 2\n90 GB/s (RDMA)\n81 GB/s (RDMA)\n12\nSM90\nCX7\nEP 8 x 4\n61 GB/s (RDMA)\n61 GB/s (RDMA)\n6\nSM100\nCX7\nEP 8 x 2\n90 GB/s (RDMA)\n91 GB/s (RDMA)\n12\nSM100\nN/A\nEP 8\n726 GB/s (NVLink)\n740 GB/s (NVLink)\n64 (Max perf)\nSM100\nN/A\nEP 8\n643 GB/s (NVLink)\n675 GB/s (NVLink)\n24 (Min #SM)\nNotes, the results are logical bandwidth. Our additions include tiled register decoding, specialized small-M buckets, direct swap epilogues, MXFP4 split-M and tail optimization, and optional single-token split-K.\nEP8 speedups across M=1--8192:\nEP8 shape\nvs\n#383\nFP8\nvs\n#440\nFP8\nvs\n#411\nMXFP4\nFlash\n1.08--1.52x\n1.08--2.04x\n1.10--1.44x\nPro\n1.12--1.48x\n1.32--1.89x\n1.11--1.44x\n2. implementation introduced in\ndeepseek-ai/DeepGEMM#383\nand the SM90 FP8\u00d7NVFP4 work in\nAichenF/DeepGEMM@megamoe_nvfp4_dev_m\n, this PR adds an optimized FP8\u00d7MXFP4 MegaMoE backend for SM90 Keeping routed-expert weights in MXFP4 enables native reuse of Hugging Face MXFP4 MoE checkpoints, while substantially reducing GPU memory pressure by avoiding a persistent FP8 weight expansion.\nSummary\nUnified SM90 persistent execution.\nUnlike\n[SM90] Add FP8 MegaMoE support\n#383\n, this implementation fuses dispatch, routed/shared L1 and L2, and combine into one persistent kernel. It adapts the SM100 ring-buffer and wave-interleaved scheduler to a cluster-free Hopper design using register-resident WGMMA, one CTA per task, and two resident CTAs per SM.\nHumming-inspired MXFP4\u00d7FP8 compute path.\nRouted weights remain packed MXFP4 in global memory. Model-load preprocessing rebases the K32 UE8M0 exponents into bounded relative scales plus one FP32 secondary scale per expert, following the fused MXFP4\u00d7FP8 approach used by Humming. During execution, packed E2M1 tiles are loaded into compact shared-memory staging, decoded and expanded on the fly into E4M3 FP8 tiles, and then consumed by Hopper WGMMA. This avoids materializing a persistent FP8 copy of the routed weights.\nBenchmark Results\nThe benchmark follows the H20 workload shapes and timing methodology from\n#383\n. thread-local mode. If the first device query happens inside a capture, it fails with\ncudaErrorStreamCaptureUnsupported\n(900), DeepJIT panics, and the capture is invalidated (\ncudaErrorStreamCaptureInvalidated\n, 901, at\ncudaStreamEndCapture\n).\nLibraries hit this through lazy initialization: the runtime created by\ncreate_lazy_jit\nqueries the device on first use. With DeepGEMM\nmain\n(which pins DeepJIT\ne5bdee2\n), the first DeepGEMM call in a process fails if it happens inside\ntorch.cuda.graph\nwith the default capture mode:\nPanic error (third-party/deep_jit/include/deep_jit/backend/cuda/device.hpp:15): cudaFree(nullptr) failed with CUDA error 900 (cudaErrorStreamCaptureUnsupported): operation not permitted when stream is capturing\n  #0 deep_jit::cuda::Device::get_prop() at third-party/deep_jit/include/deep_jit/backend/cuda/device.hpp:15\n  #1 deep_jit::cuda::CompilerOptions::default_options(deep_jit::Env const&, deep_jit::cuda::Device&) at third-party/deep_jit/include/deep_jit/backend/cuda/device.hpp:62\n  #2 ... deep_gemm::init_jit(...)::{lambda} ...\n  #3 deep_jit::LazyInit<deep_jit::Runtime<deep_jit::CUDA> >::operator->()\nThis is the only blocker I found. Once the runtime has been initialized, compiling and loading a new kernel during a global-mode capture already works.\nFix\nAdd a small RAII guard that switches the calling thread to\ncudaStreamCaptureModeRelaxed\nwith\ncudaThreadExchangeStreamCaptureMode\n, and use it only around the one-time initialization in\nget_prop()\n. The previous mode is restored when the guard goes out of scope. This is the same mechanism as PyTorch's\nc10::cuda::CUDAStreamCaptureModeGuard\n, kept local so that\ndevice.hpp\ndoes not depend on c10.\nTest\nAdds a\nCUDA device during graph capture\ncase to the CUDA test project:\nA fresh\nDevice\nis queried for the first time inside a global, thread-local and relaxed capture.\nA runtime from\ncreate_lazy_jit\nis used for the first time inside a global capture.\nEach case checks that the capture is still valid when it ends, and that the thread's capture mode is restored.\nTesting\nDiagnosis on H200\n(SM90, CUDA 13.0, PyTorch 2.11.0+cu130), DeepGEMM\nmain\n78b6900 with DeepJIT\ne5bdee2\n, before this change. Each case runs in a fresh process. \"Cold\" starts from an empty disk cache, \"warm\" from a cache filled by another process. The cluster path is enabled from a vocabulary size of 128K, based on measurements on an 8\u00d7H20 machine. SM100 and SM103 continue to use the existing 16-CTA cluster and 512K threshold.\r\n\r\n## Changes\r\n\r\n- Add `DEEP_SELECT_CUDA_ARCHS` to select `90a`, `100a`, and/or `103a` at build time.\r\n- Keep the default build targets unchanged: `sm_100a` and `sm_103a`.\r\n- Add eight SM90 cluster instantiations covering:\r\n  - `int32` and `int64` output indices\r\n  - `sorted_index` enabled and disabled\r\n  - `return_value` enabled and disabled\r\n- Dispatch SM90 BF16 workloads to an 8-CTA cluster when:\r\n  - `batch_size <= 6`\r\n  - `vocab_size >= 128K`\r\n  - `topk <= 1024`\r\n- Preserve the existing 16-CTA cluster and 512K threshold for SM100/SM103.\r\n- Fall back to `nvidia-smi -L` for CUDA platform detection when `lspci` is unavailable in a container.\r\n- Avoid the `std::format` dependency in the C++ binding so the extension builds with older host standard libraries commonly found in CUDA containers.\r\n- Document the SM90 build command:\r\n\r\n```bash\r\nDEEP_SELECT_CUDA_ARCHS=90a pip install -v .\r\n```\r\n\r\n## Performance\r\n\r\nMeasured on NVIDIA H20 with BF16 input, `batch_size` 1 and 6, and `topk` 512 and 1024. Each result is the median of 100 cold-cache runs.\r\n\r\nThe improvement column compares the new 8-CTA cluster with the existing single-CTA path compiled for SM90.\r\n\r\n| Vocabulary size | Improvement over single CTA | Speedup over `torch.topk` |\r\n|---:|---:|---:|\r\n| 128K | 25.2%\u201335.0% | 3.11\u00d7\u20133.83\u00d7 |\r\n| 256K | 36.5%\u201344.6% | 2.79\u00d7\u20133.57\u00d7 |\r\n| 512K | 52.4%\u201356.1% | 2.66\u00d7\u20133.75\u00d7 |\r\n| 1M | 61.5%\u201362.3% | 2.12\u00d7\u20134.11\u00d7 |\r\n\r\nShort-sequence and large-batch workloads continue to use the existing normal kernel. "github:deepseek-ai/DeepSpec:63:live-page", "source": "github", "kind": "resource", "url": "https://github.com/deepseek-ai/DeepSpec/pull/63", "title": "Add Sequential Temperature Scaling for confidence-head calibration by DaoyuanLi2816 \u00b7 Pull Request #63 \u00b7 deepseek-ai/DeepSpec \u00b7 GitHub", "created_at": null, "updated_at": null, "fetched_at": "2026-10-20T12:16:49Z", "state": "Conversation\nDaoyuanLi2816\ncommented\nJul 9, 2026\nCopy link\nCopy Markdown\nContributor\nWhat\nThe DSpark paper (Section 3.2.1) calibrates the confidence head with\nSequential Temperature Scaling (STS)\n: for each block position, left to right, a 1D grid search picks the temperature that minimizes the ECE of the cumulative prefix-survival probability, keeping the already-fitted earlier positions fixed. The evaluator already measures exactly that quantity (per-position cumulative-product ECE / AUROC / reliability diagrams), but the calibration step itself was missing \u2014 and the raw confidence scores are noticeably overconfident.\nThis PR adds the full offline STS workflow (pure additions, default behavior unchanged):\neval.py --confidence-dump-dir\n\u2014 dumps per-proposal confidence records to\n<dir>/<dataset>/confidence_records.jsonl\nfor offline fitting (run with\n--confidence-threshold 0\nand no calibration so the dumped logits are raw and blocks untruncated).\nscripts/fit_confidence_calibration.py\n\u2014 fits per-position temperatures from dumped records via the paper's left-to-right grid search (the ECE binning mirrors the evaluator's coarse bins), and reports cumulative ECE before/after at each position.\neval.py --confidence-calibration-path\n\u2014 applies the fitted temperatures to the confidence logits during evaluation, so both the reported calibration metrics and the\n--confidence-threshold\nearly stop consume calibrated probabilities.\nValidation\nSingle RTX 4080,\ndeepseek-ai/dspark_qwen3_4b_block7\non\nQwen/Qwen3-4B\n, temperature 1.0, threshold 0,\nmax-new-tokens\n256. Fitted on gsm8k (120 samples, 4.7k proposals); held-out datasets were never seen during fitting. probabilities\nCast logits to float32 before temperature scaling and softmax in\nsample_tokens\nRemoves a speculative-decoding distribution mismatch\ndeepspec/utils/optim.py\nWarmup wrapper delegated to\nCosineAnnealingLR.get_lr()\nin a bad handoff state\nReturn the configured peak/base LRs directly at the handoff\nPrevents LR overshoot above the configured Each change is isolated in its own commit.\n1. Draft sampling dtype mismatch\nlogits_to_probs\ncomputes the draft distribution as:\np\n=\nsoftmax\n(\nlogits\n.\nfloat\n()\n/\nT\n)\nand uses that float32\np\nin the speculative acceptance ratio\nmin(1, q / p)\nand correction distribution. Before this PR,\nsample_tokens\nsampled through the native-dtype path instead, so bfloat16 logits produced a bfloat16 probability tensor passed to\ntorch.multinomial\n. In effect, the draft token was drawn from a represented distribution\nr\n, but acceptance and correction were computed as if it came from\np\n.\nFor a one-token speculative step, with sample distribution\nr\nbut acceptance math using\np\n:\na_y = min(1, q_y / p_y)\nc_y = (q_y - p_y)_+ / sum_z (q_z - p_z)_+\nPr[out = y] = r_y a_y + (sum_x r_x (1 - a_x)) c_y\nWhen\nr = p\n, this collapses to the target distribution\nq\n. When\nr != p\n, it generally does not.\nA minimal non-degenerate example demonstrates the final marginal bias:\np = [0.3, 0.7]\nr = [0.30078125, 0.69921875]\nq = [0.4, 0.6]\n\na = [1, 0.6 / 0.7]\nc = [1, 0]\n\nPr[out = 0] = 0.30078125 + 0.69921875 * (1 - 0.6 / 0.7)\n            = 0.40066964\nq[0]        = 0.40000000\nThe exact bf16 error bound is not the important part of the fix, but the scale is enough to matter: bf16 has 7 explicit mantissa bits, spacing near 1 of\n2^-7 ~= 0.78%\n, and round-to-nearest error on the order of\n2^-8 ~= 0.39%\nfor normal-range values.\nThis PR changes\nsample_tokens\nto cast before temperature scaling and softmax:\nflat_logits\n=\nlogits\n.\nreshape\n(\n-\n1\n,\nvocab_size\n).\nfloat\n()\n/\ntemperature\nThat makes the sampled draft distribution match the\np\nused by rejection sampling.\n2. Warmup to cosine LR overshoot\nThis is a wrapper state-transition bug, not ordinary\nCosineAnnealingLR\nbehavior.\nAt the warmup/cosine handoff, the old wrapper delegated to the cosine scheduler's chainable\nget_lr()\npath with:\ncosine.last_epoch == 0\ncosine._is_initial == False\noptimizer.lr == warmup_peak\nThat is not the ordinary closed-form epoch-0 cosine value. The recursive update applies:\nLR = eta_min + 2 / (1 + cos(pi / T_max)) * (LR_current - eta_min)\nWith\neta_min = 0\nand\nLR_current = peak_lr\n, the transition becomes:\nLR_transition = 2 / (1 + cos(pi / T_max)) * peak_lr\nSince\ncos(pi / T_max) < 1\nfor\nT_max > 1\n, this is above the configured peak. Examples:\nT_max\nfactor\novershoot\n5\n1.10557\n+10.56%\n10\n1.02509\n+2.51%\n50\n1.00099\n+0.0988%\n100\n1.00025\n+0.0247%\nThis PR special-cases the handoff to return the configured peak/base LRs directly instead of calling the recursive\nget_lr()\nbranch at\nlast_epoch == 0\n. The following scheduler step then advances cosine normally from epoch 0 to epoch 1.\n3. Eagle3 fallback mask crash\nprepare_4d_causal_attention_mask\nneeds the additive padding-mask key dimension to equal\nkv_len\n.\nAt TTT step\nk >= 1\n:\nq_len = seq_len\npast_seen_tokens = k * seq_len\nkv_len = past_seen_tokens + seq_len = (k + 1) * seq_len\nThe causal mask has shape\n[1, 1, q_len, kv_len]\n, but the incoming 2D\nattention_mask\ncovers one fixed-size chunk, so it has shape\n[B, seq_len]\n. Expanding or slicing it cannot create cached-key columns:\ncausal:        [1, 1, seq_len, (k + 1) * seq_len]\nexpanded_mask: [B, 1, 1, seq_len]\nAdding those tensors fails on dim 3 for every cached step where\nkv_len != seq_len\n. For\nseq_len = 4, k = 1\n, this is\n[1,1,4,8] + [B,1,1,4]\n.\nThe initial fix padded the missing columns as valid, but that was too broad: if earlier cached chunks include padded positions, it would incorrectly make those cached padding slots attendable. This PR now extends the keep-mask by repeating the fixed chunk mask pattern until it reaches\nkv_len\n, then slices to\nkv_len\n. That fixes the shape invariant while preserving padded columns in cached chunks. 2026\n\u2022\nedited\nLoading\nUh oh!\nThere was an error while loading.\nPlease reload this page\n.\nCopy link\nCopy Markdown\nSummary\nThis PR adds an opt-in, two-stage packed selected-KV sparse decode path for the\nGLM-5.2 / V3.2 FP8 MLA format on SM90:\npack_selected_kv\ngathers selected records from the paged KV cache into an\nopaque tile-major workspace.\nflash_mla_with_packed_kvcache\nconsumes that workspace with a packed\nsparse-decode specialization that avoids sparse index and page translation\non the attention critical path.\nThe existing native sparse-decode API and dispatch remain unchanged. The new\npath is explicitly restricted to SM90 and is not enabled automatically.\nNote\nAlternative host-offload path (not part of this pr):\nThe packed interface also enables a separate offload path in which gathering and packing are performed on the host, followed by transferring only the packed selected-KV workspace to the device. In this case, the full KV caches of the shared layers no longer need to remain resident in HBM. This can substantially expand KV-cache capacity.\nSince this implementation introduces a compile-time packed-KV specialization in the existing SM90 sparse decode kernel, I\u2019d appreciate maintainers\u2019 guidance on whether you prefer the current shared-kernel approach, a policy/loader-based abstraction, or a separate packed-kernel implementation; I\u2019m happy to restructure it in a follow-up revision based on your feedback.\nMotivation\nGLM-5.2 can share selected physical indices across multiple MLA layers. When\nthose indices are available before the layer that consumes them, the runtime\ncan move sparse address resolution and KV gathering into an earlier overlap\nwindow.\nThe attention kernel can then read a contiguous packed workspace instead of\nrepeatedly:\nloading and validating physical indices;\ntranslating indices into page and in-page coordinates;\nconstructing unrelated global addresses; and\ngathering NoPE, scale, and RoPE components from the paged cache.\nPacking and attention are intentionally separate operations so a runtime can\noverlap packing with preceding work such as MoE communication.\nWhat changed\nSM90 packing kernel\npack_selected_kv\ncopies each selected 656-byte V3.2 record byte-for-byte:\n512-byte FP8 E4M3 NoPE data;\n16-byte FP32 scales; and\n128-byte BF16 RoPE data.\nThe packer writes an opaque 64-token tile-major workspace. It supports a valid\nselected-index prefix described by\ntopk_length\nand zero-fills invalid suffix\nrecords.\nCallers may provide reusable contiguous\nuint8\nstorage using\nget_packed_kv_workspace_size\n.\nPacked attention specialization\nThe packed H=64 and H=128 instantiations reuse FlashMLA's existing:\nWGMMA and dequantization pipeline;\nonline softmax;\nDSM clustering for H=128;\nsplit accumulation;\nepilogue; and\ncombine kernel.\nThe specialization replaces the sparse index/page/gather loader with direct\ntile-major loads and preserves partial-tail masking.\nScheduler policy\nH=128 uses the same scheduler as native sparse decode.\nH=64 uses the compact request-local scheduler selected by the L20X\nexperiments. For the measured full-prefix\ntopk=2048\n, 132-SM\nB*S_q=16\nregime, it falls back to the faster native scheduler.\nThis scheduler is used only by the opt-in packed helper and does not change\nnative sparse decode. The benchmark includes an identical-scheduler control to\nisolate the packed-load kernel gain.\nPython API\nThis PR adds:\nget_packed_kv_workspace_size\nget_packed_mla_metadata\npack_selected_kv\nflash_mla_with_packed_kvcache\nSupported scope\nThe initial path is intentionally limited to:\nSM90;\nV3.2 FP8 sparse KV records;\nd_qk=576\n;\nd_v=512\n;\nH in {64, 128}\n;\nMQA KV; and\ntop-k capacity divisible by 64.\nThe API fails closed for unsupported architectures and shapes. SM100 is not\nenabled because this implementation has only been evaluated on SM90.\nL20X results\nThe repository-local benchmark was run on an NVIDIA L20X with 132 SMs using:\nCUDA 13.0;\nPyTorch 2.13.0;\ntopk=2048\n;\nB={1,2,4,7,8}\n;\nS_q={1,2,3,4,8,16}\n;\nH={64,128}\n;\nrequest-disjoint physical KV ranges;\na 512 MiB L2 flush before each measured path;\n10 warmups and 200 timed repetitions; and\n60 total cases.\nComparison\nGeometric-mean speedup\nWins\nPacked attention, identical scheduler\n1.098x\n60/60\nIntegrated packed attention stage\n1.115x\n60/60\nIntegrated H=64 cohort\n1.131x\n30/30\nH=128 cohort, native scheduler\n1.100x\n30/30\nPack + attention without overlap\n0.740x\n0/60\nPacking is excluded from the attention-stage comparisons.\nThe unhidden result is included deliberately: this path is useful only when\nthe runtime can move most packing work outside the attention critical path.\nThis PR does not add automatic dispatch or change the default native path.\nThe H=64 hybrid policy selected the tuned scheduler in 26 cases and the native\nfallback in four cases. The four fallback cases improved by approximately\n6.5% to 7.4% compared with using the tuned scheduler unconditionally.\nCorrectness and benchmark coverage\npython tests/test_", "body_excerpted": link\nCopy Markdown\nCollaborator\n\ud83e\udd16 ds-review-bot Code Review\nv6\nThe inference path now applies the RMSNorm weight fusion consistently with the grad-enabled path, and the added tests cover both weighted and unweighted cases.\nv4\nThe MR fixes bug\n#22\n: the no-grad (inference)\nmhc_pre\npath sent the RAW\nfn\nweight into the fused\nmhc_pre_big_fuse\nkernel (which has no\nnorm_weight\nargument) and therefore SILENTLY DROPPED the optional RMSNorm\nnorm_weight\napplied by the grad-enabled path. The fix folds\nnorm_weight\ninto\nfn\nBEFORE the fused GEMM: since the weight is constant during inference,\n(residual * norm_weight) @ fn == residual @ (fn * norm_weight)\n, and the fused kernel divides by the SAME (unweighted) rms-denominator as the grad path and the torch reference; dtype/round-to-tf32 handling also match, so the math is CORRECT. A regression test is added that compares grad vs no-grad outputs (with/without\nnorm_weight\n) over\ntorch.is_grad_enabled()\n. Main concerns: (1) the test uses an extremely tight\natol/rtol = 1e-6\nbetween two op-fusion/rounding/reduction-order-DIFFERENT kernels plus very large shapes, risking flaky/over-strict failures and slow CI; (2) the fusion logic is DUPLICATED/inlined at a low level across the two paths, which is itself the kind of divergence that caused\n#22\n(the grad path wraps the same\nfn * norm_weight\nmerge in an autograd Function). Cleaning this into one helper selecting the autograd/raw-forward merge on\ntorch.is_grad_enabled()\nwould both remove the duplication and prevent future divergence. Overall: correct in substance, but I would request minor changes to test tolerance/duplication.\nv3\nThis PR fixes a correctness bug in the no-grad (inference) path of\nmhc_pre\nin\ntile_kernels/modeling/mhc/functional.py\n. Previously, when\ntorch.is_grad_enabled()\nwas False, the code called\nmhc_pre_big_fuse(...)\nwithout ever applying\nnorm_weight\n, since the fused kernel does not accept a\nnorm_weight\nargument. As a result the RMSNorm weight was silently dropped in inference mode, diverging from the grad path (which fuses it via\n_MHCFnNormwMerge\ninside\nmhc_pre_norm_fn\n). The fix merges\nnorm_weight\ninto\nfn\nbefore calling the fused kernel using the same\n_mhc_fn_normw_merge_fwd\nkernel used by the grad path, so both paths are now numerically consistent.\nThe fix is correct and minimal: it mirrors the existing grad-path implementation exactly (\ntorch.empty_like(fn)\n+\n_mhc_fn_normw_merge_fwd(*fn.shape)(fn, norm_weight, merged_fn)\n), so inference and training now produce identical results. This directly exercises the bug and is an appropriate regression guard (a self-consistency check between the two code paths, where the grad path was the known-correct reference). The official model card recommends deepseek-recipe and invites issues or contact at service@deepseek.com. Our failed runs used vLLM's V4.1 encoder, not deepseek-recipe itself; this is **not a claim that recipe caused the loops**. Please redirect us if another official tracker is preferred.\n\n## Environment and workload\n\n- Model: DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash open weights. This is a local-inference report, not a demonstrated failure of the official hosted API.\n- Engine in the reported Spark experiments: vLLM 0.28.1rc1.dev388+g8a728663c.\n- Hardware: four NVIDIA DGX Spark / GB10 nodes forming one TP4/DCP4 model service.\n- Recorded precision: BF16 engine dtype, MXFP4 routed-expert weights, fp8_ds_mla main attention KV. These refer to different objects.\n- Main fixed replay input: 107 messages, 26 tool definitions, 74,657 actual input tokens.\n- Sampling in the completed five-arm experiment: thinking enabled, numeric reasoning_effort=100, temperature=1.0, top_p=0.95, min_p=0, top_k=-1, max_tokens=8192, global concurrency 4.\n- DSpark enabled in that experiment: num_speculative_tokens=5, probabilistic draft sampling, block rejection sampling, adaptive verification disabled.\n- A thinking-budget sampler fix was present in the budget experiment; those results are implementation-specific mitigation evidence, not an unmodified-vLLM or official parameter recommendation.\n\n## Expected vs. actual behavior\n\nExpected: complete the reasoning phase and emit valid tool calls or a final answer.\n\nActual native output contains repeated procedural phrases, for example:\n\n```text\nWriting.\nLet me output.\nGo.\nOK.\nWriting.\nLet me write.\nGo.\nNow.\n```\n\nFor baseline seed 21020, the saved output contains 2,158 native generated tokens before observer cancellation, 8,262 reasoning characters, empty content, and no tool calls. An exact 20-token period starts at generated position 1,987 (one-based) and repeats for at least eight cycles. We rechecked equality directly against the saved token IDs:\n\n```json\n[339,46837,339,5718,678,5238,339,5188,339,11932,339,46837,339,5718,678,5085,339,5188,339,8197]\n```\n\nThe five confirmed baseline loops were independently rechecked:\n\n| Seed | Exact-cycle onset, generated token position (1-based) | Period, tokens | Consecutive cycles verified |\n|---:|---:|---:|---:|\n| 21020 | 1987 | 20 | 8 |\n| 21044 | 2418 | 15 | 10 |\n| 21054 | 2308 | 12 | 10 |\n| 21068 | 2916 | 15 | 8 |\n| 21098 | 3438 | 13 | 9 |\n\nThese are engine-token loops within one generation. Client request repetition is not needed to produce these captured cycles.\n\n## Controlled observations\n\n### 1. Fixed-input, five-arm experiment\n\nEach arm dispatched exactly 100 requests; sent failures were not replaced.\n\n| Arm, single parameter change | Complete non-repeat responses | Confirmed repeats | Service/transport unknowns |\n|---|---:|---:|---:|\n| A: no thinking budget or penalty | 88 | 5 | 7 |\n| B: thinking budget 1536 | 92 | 0 | 8 |\n| C: frequency_penalty=0.2 | 88 | 0 | 12 |\n| D: presence_penalty=0.2 | 88 | 5 | 7 |\n| E: repetition_penalty=1.05 | 92 | 2 | 6 |\n\nUnknowns are not clean negatives. A complete response does not prove business-task success. A separate 100-task quality evaluation per arm scored A/B/C/D/E as 91/93/84/91/92 passes; service failures remain in the denominators, so these numbers do not establish quality-neutral mitigation.\n\n### 2. Identical input IDs, different generation trajectories\n\nWe compared one normal baseline seed (21000), the five looping baseline seeds above, and their six budget-arm counterparts. All 12 captured prompt-token arrays were identical, length 74,657.\n\nFor the five looping baseline seeds, the corresponding budget-arm responses completed with tool calls. Their reasoning lengths were 1536, 175, 341, 299, and 286 tokens respectively. Only one reached the budget. The four early completions cannot all be explained by forcibly cutting off an existing loop at 1536. We have not established why these trajectories diverge before budget exhaustion.\n\n### 3. DSpark / CUDA-graph pilot\n\nA separate 16-request, effort50 pilot yielded phrase-threshold repeat cancellations:\n\n| DSpark | Graph mode | Repeat cancellations / attempts |\n|---|---|---:|\n| enabled | FULL_AND_PIECEWISE | 2/4 |\n| disabled | NONE | 2/4 |\n| disabled | FULL_AND_PIECEWISE | 1/4 |\n| enabled | NONE | 0/4 |\n\nGraph NONE here is not a claim that enforce-eager was used. This small pilot cannot estimate production incidence, but neither DSpark nor graph execution is a necessary condition for the observed loops.\n\n### 4. A replica-deterministic sample sets a value\n  threshold and a 256-bin scale; every CTA walks its slice once; the last-arriving CTA merges\n  the bin-major publish tables and resolves the crossing bin with an exact key-space select.\n  Rows up to 16K columns never enter that pipeline: a census arm (<= 4K) and a register-resident\n  arm (4K to 16K) solve them in one CTA. Floods past the tie capacity are refined from the\n  crossing bin's exact key range; anything else falls through to a fused exact re-solve on\n  device, so every call stays sync-free and CUDA-graph capturable.\n- `radix_primitives` \u2014 SGLang's coarse-histogram structure made exact (no tie truncation),\n  fp32/fp16/bf16, grouped rows (`next_n`) and compressed columns (`compress_ratio`), optional\n  values, multi-CTA long rows.\n- `cutlass_primitives` \u2014 the vendored cutlass-primitives library (`VENDORED.md`, Apache-2.0):\n  register, clustered-register and streaming kernels with a census split for large k; the\n  full-row leader. `flashinfer.topk_varlen` exports its workspace helpers\n  (`cutlass_primitives_workspace_bytes`, `cutlass_primitives_row_order`) and\n  `release_cutlass_primitives_resources`.\n- `sglang` \u2014 SGLang's own DeepSeek-V4 indexer top-k (Apache-2.0, vendored verbatim from upstream\n  commit 7f27bf4708; `csrc/sglang_dsv4/VENDORED.md`, `licenses/LICENSE.sglang.txt`) under the\n  same contract, including a paged variant: the same kernel SGLang runs, for parity and for the\n  check that the FlashInfer API adds no overhead over the raw kernel. It is exact only while the\n  rank-k bin holds at most 2048 candidates, so `auto` never selects it.\n\n**Fused paged output.** `top_k_varlen(..., page_table=table, page_size=64)` returns physical\nKV slots `page_table[q, c // page_size] * page_size + c % page_size` from the selection launch\nitself (the SGLang DSA decode contract, one kernel instead of a top-k plus a transform pass).\nThe table is a contiguous, 16-byte-aligned int32 `(requests, max_pages)` tensor covering every\nrequest's valid length; it may be narrower than the logits width, and rows past its coverage\nare clamped to the covered prefix. Served by `walkfirst_primitives`, `radix_primitives` and\n`sglang`; `backend=\"auto\"` with a page table ranks the two exact primitives backends (they are\noutside the unpaged ranking).\n\n**Also in this PR:** a fix to the pre-existing multi-CTA `radix` kernel (chunks shorter than\ntheir misaligned prefix read past the valid length and wrote shared memory at negative offsets;\nCTA 0 could reset the group's arrival counter while a time-sliced peer still spun on it; the\nper-group state grows by one int, STATE_SIZE 770 to 771, allocated internally with a bumped\ncache key), the trace template for `top_k_varlen` extended with the paged inputs (the page size\nnames the paged definitions), the benchmarks that produced the numbers below, the four new\nbackends in `benchmarks/routines/topk_varlen.py`, and a one-line fix to\n`tests/trace/example.py` (an upstream call passed a `str` device to `is_sm100a_supported`, which\ncrashes the example on sm_100 hosts).\n\n**Why.** The DSA indexer runs one top-k per layer per decode step; SGLang today uses its own\nfused kernel. These backends are exact where that kernel approximates, faster on every decode\ncell we measured across six architectures, and fuse the page-table output so an engine needs\none launch. please review that one first.\n\nThis makes `trtllm_moe_finalize_allreduce_fusion` faster and more accurate:\n\n- **Gather the top-k rows together.** The finalize loop handled one route at a time: load the permuted index, then\n  load that row, then move to the next route. Each row load had to wait for its own index load. Now the kernel\n  loads the indices and scales of 8 routes first, then all 8 rows, so the row loads are in flight together.\n- **Accumulate in FP32.** The routed sum, the `routed_scaling_factor`, the shared-expert add and the sum over ranks\n  were all done in the activation dtype, rounding after every step. They now stay in FP32 and round once. This\n  matches `allreduce_fusion` with `fp32_acc=True`.\n\nTest fixes in `test_trtllm_moe_allreduce_fusion_finalize.py`:\n\n- Its reference is now computed in FP32. The old BF16 reference does each multiply and add in BF16, and the\n  BF16/TP4 case failed on B300 even before this change.\n- `scale_out` is now sized for the `SWIZZLED_128x4` layout the kernel writes:\n  `round_up(T, 128) * round_up(H / 16, 4)` bytes. The test allocated `T * H / 16` elements, and the kernel wrote\n  past the end. That only showed up as an illegal address once the FP32 reference allocated tensors next to it.\n\n## \ud83d\udd0d Related Issues\n\n#2269, #3671 (fused finalize slower than finalize + `kARResidualRMSNorm`)\n\n## \ud83d\ude80 Pull Request Checklist\n\n### \u2705 Pre-commit Checks\n\n- [x] I have installed `pre-commit` by running `pip install pre-commit` (or used your preferred method).\n- [x] I have installed the hooks with `pre-commit install`.\n- [x] I have run the hooks manually with `pre-commit run --all-files` and fixed any reported issues.\n\n## \ud83e\uddea Tests\n\n- [x] Tests have been added or updated as needed.\n- [x] All tests are passing (`unittest`, etc.).\n\nB300 (SM103), TP4, CUDA 13.4. null, "content_hash": "4ffb05e0994958da3c337cd20d56988ccb169fcb126c1b6270ad8429aeed8d0e", "body": "## Before submitting\n\n- [x] I have searched existing issues and this request has not been filed yet.\n\n## Problem\n\nThe MNNVL CuTe DSL all-reduce fusion backend currently ships built-in static profiles only for GB300, BF16, `H=8192`, `Top-K=10`, and TP8/TP16. `MNNVLCuteDSLConfig.resolve()` keys profiles by TP size, hidden size, Top-K, and dtype, and raises `No MNNVL CuTe DSL profile supports this static shape` for other combinations.\n\nThis makes an otherwise promising backend difficult to use outside the initial Qwen3.8 2.4T-style geometry. In particular, inference engines cannot select it broadly for other tensor-parallel models or hardware without carrying custom profiles and doing their own correctness/performance tuning.\n\nThe backend's LL/BT/HT protocols cover decode through prefill and the initial implementation reports strong gains over the existing composed MNNVL and NCCL paths. Broadening the validated profile matrix would make those protocols usable by more models and would unblock a general vLLM integration instead of a model-specific one.\n\n## Requested outcome\n\nExpand the validated and tuned MNNVL CuTe DSL profile matrix beyond the initial GB300 `H=8192`, `Top-K=10`, TP8/TP16 configuration.\n\nConcretely, it would be useful to have:\n\n- Profiles for representative hidden sizes and model families beyond the initial geometry.\n- TP2/TP4 coverage where the protocols and topology support it, in addition to TP8/TP16.\n- Validation and tuning on SM100 and SM103, plus a clear path for architecture-specific profiles on future MNNVL systems.\n- A documented/public benchmark workflow for sweeping `M`, selecting LL/BT/HT crossover points, and producing checked-in presets.\n- A safe dispatch/fallback story so inference engines can probe support without hard-coding one model configuration.\n\n## Target hardware\n\n- SM100 (B200, GB200)\n- SM103 (B300, GB300)\n- Future architecture\n\n## Inference engine\n\n- vLLM\n- PyTorch native\n\n## Affected model or model family\n\nTensor-parallel models using MNNVL all-reduce fusion. The initial checked-in profiles correspond to Qwen3.8 2.4T-style `H=8192`, TP8/TP16 geometry; the request is to support additional public model shapes rather than another single-model specialization.\n\n## Workload and configuration\n\n- Data type and quantization: BF16 all-reduce input; fused residual add + RMSNorm, optionally preceded by MoE finalize/shared-expert add. Future quantized output fusion can be evaluated separately.\n- Batch size or request concurrency: decode through prefill; sweep the full supported `M` range so LL, BT, and HT crossover points are tuned per profile.\n- Sequence lengths or token counts: all; the relevant dispatch dimension is `M`.\n- Parallelism: TP2/TP4/TP8/TP16 over NVLink/MNNVL, subject to topology support.\n- Relevant shapes: multiple hidden sizes and Top-K values beyond `H=8192`, `Top-K=10`.\n- Environment: Blackwell SM100/SM103 first; future-architecture profiles when public hardware validation is available.\n\n## Current workaround\n\nUse the existing TRT-LLM MNNVL, NCCL, or framework-specific all-reduce backend, or maintain a private `MNNVLCuteDSLConfig` and retune all LL/BT/HT presets and route boundaries. The first options leave performance on the table, while the latter is not a maintainable integration strategy for frameworks.\n\n## Impact\n\n- Missing model or hardware support\n- Throughput\n- Latency\n- API usability or integration complexity\n- Testing, maintainability, or documentation\n\n## Acceptance criteria\n\n- Add correctness-tested built-in profiles for at least one non-`H=8192` model geometry, and add TP2/TP4 coverage where supported.\n- Exercise both fusion patterns across the newly supported profiles:\n  - AllReduce + residual add + RMSNorm\n  - MoE finalize + shared-expert add + AllReduce + residual add + RMSNorm\n- Publish benchmark results across representative `M` values and derive LL/BT/HT route boundaries from those results.\n- Preserve the existing GB300 TP8/TP16 profile performance and numerical contracts.\n- Provide a support-query or documented fallback path suitable for inference-engine dispatch.\n- Keep hardware-specific tuning isolated in profiles so future architectures can be added without model checks in framework integrations.\n\n## Related work, dependencies, or suggested scope\n\n- Initial backend: https://github.com/flashinfer-ai/flashinfer/pull/4358\n- Related but distinct PDL-overlap request: https://github.com/flashinfer-ai/flashinfer/issues/5151\n- Related TP12/Kimi K3 shape request: "2026-10-01T09:41:03Z", "updated_at": "2026-10-19T22:08:32Z", "fetched_at": "2026-10-20T12:14:33Z", "state": "closed", "merged_at": null, "parent": null, "content_hash": "4ce821c6040b2dbe33e48c51b5d15c6231a32ecddbc45b37d72e21d9b97ec857", "body": "<!-- .github/pull_request_template.md -->\n\n## \ud83d\udccc Description\n\nQwen4Exp's quantized sparse attention (QSA) for SM80 and newer, as `flashinfer.qsa_ops`, with the two pieces of common code it needs.\n\nA QSA layer scores a compressed index cache, keeps the top blocks per query, and attends over those tokens where they sit in the paged KV cache. The package has the per-step kernels (pre-indexer, paged scorer, route expansion and mapping, output gate) and three objects that run them out of caller-owned workspaces, so a captured CUDA graph can replay a whole step: `QSASelection`, `QSAAttention` and `QSA`. `qsa_capabilities()` tells a serving stack what the build carries.\n\nTwo changes to existing code support it:\n\n- `BlockSparseAttentionWrapper` runs on a paged KV cache (`plan(..., kv_cache_page_size=...)`; dense, FP8 or NVFP4; NHD or HND) and reports its workspace size before planning, so several live plans can share one arena. It also fixes the default FP8 scales, which were sized by the block width instead of the KV-head count.\n- `top_k_ragged_transform` takes `out`, `workspace` and a resolved `backend`, so a caller can run it without allocating and with the backend fixed up front. Calls without them are unchanged.\n\nThe kernels run on SM80 and every later architecture: the JIT and AOT builds target 8.x through 12.x, and there are no architecture-specific paths, so newer GPUs run the same mma.sync and cp.async code rather than tuned variants. SM7x is excluded because the scorer's m16n8k16 arrived with SM80. The pre-indexer, scorer and route are one JIT module; the gate is a second one built without fast math, so a subnormal logistic is not flushed to zero. `cp_async.cuh` gains one L1-caching copy and `mma.cuh` the m16n8k16 helpers; existing functions are untouched.\n\n#5859, #5860 and #5861 were folded into this PR and closed.\n\n## \ud83d\udd0d Related Issues\n\nTracked in #5669.\n\n## \ud83d\ude80 Pull Request Checklist\n\nThank you for contributing to FlashInfer! The paged block-sparse route is checked against dense attention over the same entries for bf16, FP8 and NVFP4 caches, and the top-k caller-buffer path against the per-call transform. On sm_80 everything in `tests/qsa_ops`, `tests/attention/test_block_sparse.py`, `tests/utils/test_topk.py` and the trace tests passes; the SM90-only FP8 default-scale test skips. Nothing has run on SM90 or newer.\n\n## \ud83d\udd2c Experimental Track\n\n<!-- Only for PRs submitted under the experimental policy (CONTRIBUTING.md \u2192 \"Experimental APIs and Backends\").\n     Leave this section untouched for normal PRs. -->\n\n- [ ] This PR is **experimental**: it adds or changes code under `flashinfer/experimental/` and/or an `@flashinfer_experimental_api`. "6e73e3656eda552a2fecd4008a6c6e72fc68735511c6ca71f6fa92438ab5a90d", "body": "## Summary\n\nEach recipe page now lists the **people who wrote it**: GitHub avatars in the footer, most commits first, with links to the file's history. History links appear only for pages that have commits: the owning repo, and the old `models/` path here for recipes that moved. The JSON API carries the same list as `contributors`. The credit comes from git history, so authors fill in nothing.\n\n### How credit is computed\n`scripts/fetch-contributors.mjs` asks the GitHub GraphQL API for the commits that touched each recipe:\n- in its owning repo, e.g. `vllm-project/vllm:recipes/models/\u2026`;\n- in any overlay;\n- at its old `models/<hf_id>.yaml` path here, so history from before the move to vLLM still counts.\n\nCommit authors and `Co-authored-by:` trailers count, matched to GitHub accounts by email. Not counted:\n- **Bots and coding agents**, such as the `claude`, `codex` and `cursoragent` co-author trailers.\n- **Commits touching more than 20 recipes**, i.e. catalog-wide maintenance like field renames, version bumps or `date_added`. The exception is the commit that created a file, which always counts, so every recipe credits its author.\n- **Commits that only copy recipes between repos**: vllm-project/vllm#61302 and the `[Doc] Sync \u2026 vllm-project/recipes\u2026` syncs. A change is credited where it was first made.\n\nThe result, `recipe-contributors.json` (hf_id \u2192 `{ contributors, history }`), is committed. A new workflow, `recipe-contributors.yml`, refreshes it every 6 hours and on `models/**` pushes, and commits any change as the bot. CONTRIBUTING has a short \"Credit\" section that explains the rules.\n\n### Current data\n- 215 recipes, 148 contributors; every recipe credits at least one person.\n- One GraphQL run covers 401 file histories in about 40 seconds.\n- Example, DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash: functionstackx (6), Fangzhou-Ai (5), esmeetu (4), hshrivastava-droid (4), and 10 more.\n\n## Test plan\n- [x] `node scripts/fetch-contributors.mjs` against the live API, with the results spot-checked by hand.
